```rust
pub fn stage2(config: &Config, mut mesh: ConnectedMesh, viz: Option<&VizSender>) -> Result<Stage2Output, Stage2Error>
```

**Flow**:
1. **Stage 2.1**: Call `deduce_planar_hypotheses()` → returns `Vec<PlanarHypothesis>`
2. **Stage 2.2**: Call `deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses()` → returns `Vec<CylindricalHypothesis>`
3. **Stage 2.3**: Call `deduce_spherical_hypotheses()` → returns `Vec<SphericalHypothesis>`
4. **Stage 2.4**: Ruled surfaces (not implemented)
5. **Stage 2.5**: NURBS surfaces (not implemented)
6. **Stage 2.6**: Call `select_surfaces()` → greedy area-based selection
7. **Compare phase**: Optional validation against reference STEP file for each stage
8. **Return**: `Stage2Output` struct containing mesh + all hypotheses + selected surfaces

## **Conical Test Models**

**1. Simple Cone** [tests/ccad/parts/simple_cone.lua](tests/ccad/parts/simple_cone.lua):
- Frustum (truncated cone): bottom diameter=20, top diameter=10, height=30
- Expected: 1 convex conical + 2 planar caps
- Half-angle: atan(1/6) ≈ 9.46°

**2. Block with Conical Hole** [tests/ccad/parts/block_with_conical_hole.lua](tests/ccad/parts/block_with_conical_hole.lua):
- Rectangular block 30×30×20 with centered concave conical through-hole
- Cone: bottom diameter=12, top diameter=8, height=22
- Expected: 6 planar + 1 concave conical
- Half-angle: atan(0.1) ≈ 5.71°

**3. Cone-Cylinder Junction** [tests/ccad/parts/cone_cylinder.lua](tests/ccad/parts/cone_cylinder.lua):
- Frustum (20→10 diameter, height=15) + cylinder (diameter=10, height=15)
- Tangent junction where cone top matches cylinder diameter
- Expected: 1 convex conical + 1 convex cylindrical + 2 planar caps
- Cone half-angle: atan(1/3) ≈ 18.43°

Generated STEP/STL files exist in `tests/ccad/generated/` with corresponding names.

```
//! Stage 2: Surface Fitting
//!
//! 2.1: Deduce planar hypotheses
//! 2.2: Deduce cylindrical hypotheses
//! 2.3: Deduce spherical hypotheses
//! 2.4: Deduce ruled surface hypotheses
//! 2.5: Deduce NURBS hypotheses
//! 2.6: Select surfaces for reconstruction

mod cylindrical;
mod planar;
mod spherical;

use crate::config::Config;
use crate::stage1::{self, ConnectedMesh, MeshFace, MeshVertex};
use crate::viz::{self, VizAction, VizSender};
use opencascade_sys::gp;
use std::collections::HashSet;
use std::time::Instant;
use std::error::Error;
use std::fmt::{Display, Formatter};

use self::cylindrical::{
    compare_cylindrical_hypotheses, deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses, vertex_to_cylinder_distance,
};
use self::planar::{compare_planar_hypotheses, deduce_planar_hypotheses};
use self::spherical::{
    bounding_box_diagonal, compare_spherical_hypotheses, deduce_spherical_hypotheses,
    vertex_to_sphere_distance,
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Constants
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Lower bound on cross product magnitude |n₁ × n₂| for a valid cylinder/sphere
/// seed pair. Since |n₁ × n₂| = sin(θ) for unit normals, this corresponds to a
/// minimum dihedral angle of arcsin(0.01) ≈ 0.57° (~0.6°). Seed pairs closer
/// than this are too nearly parallel for numerically stable axis estimation
/// (cylinders) or sphere fitting (spheres). Together with `angular_tol` (the
/// upper bound, typically 17.5°), this defines the valid seed-pair window
/// [~0.6°, angular_tol].
pub(crate) const MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD: f64 = 0.01;

/// Fast-reject multiplier for BFS vertex-distance checks. When a candidate face
/// has a vertex farther than `REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER × vertex_tol` from the
/// current fitted surface, the distance is too large for a re-fit to absorb and
/// the face is rejected immediately without attempting re-fitting.
pub(crate) const REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER: f64 = 2.0;

/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a cylindrical hypothesis.
/// Any real CAD tessellation of a cylinder produces at least 3 facets around
/// the circumference; this rejects spurious 2-face fits (e.g. adjacent torus
/// facets that locally approximate a cylinder).
pub(crate) const MIN_CYLINDER_FACES: usize = 3;

/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a spherical hypothesis.
/// Sphere fitting has 4 degrees of freedom (cx, cy, cz, r), so at least 4
/// non-degenerate faces are needed for a well-determined fit. This also rejects
/// spurious fits from small patches that are consistent with many surface types.
pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_FACES: usize = 4;

/// Maximum sphere radius as a multiple of the mesh bounding-box diagonal.
/// With solid-angle coverage validation and surface-tolerance validation during
/// BFS, this no longer needs to be tight — those checks prevent pathological
/// growth. A large value serves as a numerical guardrail against degenerate fits.
pub(crate) const MAX_SPHERE_RADIUS_FACTOR: f64 = 1000.0;

/// Minimum eigenvalue ratio for solid-angle coverage validation.
/// Centroid-to-center direction vectors of a genuine spherical hypothesis span
/// 3D, so all eigenvalues of their covariance matrix are substantial.
/// Fillet-strip growth produces nearly coplanar directions (λ₃ ≈ 0).
pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_EIGENVALUE_RATIO: f64 = 0.01;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Hypothesis data structures
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A planar surface hypothesis fitted to a set of mesh faces.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PlanarHypothesis {
    /// Unit normal vector pointing outward from the shell/solid.
    pub normal: [f64; 3],
    /// Signed distance from origin to plane along the normal.
    pub distance: f64,
    /// Set of mesh face indices that fit this hypothesis.
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
    /// Set of
```

```
//! Stage 1: Mesh Input & Preprocessing
//!
//! 1.1: Read STL file into ConnectedMesh with welded vertices.
//! 1.2: Validate mesh topology, compute normals and neighbors.

use crate::config::Config;
use opencascade_sys::{b_rep_builder_api, b_rep_extrema, extrema, gp, message, rw_stl};
use std::collections::{HashMap, HashSet, VecDeque};
use std::time::Instant;
use std::error::Error;
use std::fmt::{Display, Formatter};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Constants
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

pub const UNDEDUCED_PLANAR_HYPOTHESIS: i32 = -2;
pub const NO_HYPOTHESIS: i32 = -1;
pub const UNDEDUCED_CYLINDRICAL_HYPOTHESIS: i32 = -2;
pub const UNDEDUCED_SPHERICAL_HYPOTHESIS: i32 = -2;

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Data structures — Stage 1 output
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct MeshVertex {
    pub x: f64,
    pub y: f64,
    pub z: f64,
}

impl MeshVertex {
    pub fn from_xyz(x: f64, y: f64, z: f64) -> Self {
        Self { x, y, z }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct MeshFace {
    pub vertex_count: u8,
    pub vertex_indices: [usize; 4],
    pub neighbors: [i32; 4],
    pub normal: Option<[f64; 3]>,
    pub planar_hypothesis: i32,
    pub cylindrical_hypothesis: i32,
    pub spherical_hypothesis: i32,
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
```

```
pub fn stage2(config: &Config, mut mesh: ConnectedMesh, viz: Option<&crate::viz::VizSender>) -> Result<Stage2Output, Stage2Error> {
    // Stage 2.1: Deduce planar hypotheses
    let t = Instant::now();
    let viz_21 = if config.viz_active(2, 1) { viz } else { None };
    let (mut planar_hypotheses, planar_quit) = deduce_planar_hypotheses(&mut mesh, config.vertex_tolerance_mm, config.verbosity, viz_21);

    if !config.quiet {
        let multi_face_count = planar_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| h.faces.len() > 1).count();
        let single_face_count = planar_hypotheses.len() - multi_face_count;
        let covered_faces: usize = planar_hypotheses.iter().map(|h| h.faces.len()).sum();
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.1 ({:.3}s): Deduced {} planar hypotheses ({} multi-face, {} single-face) covering {} of {} mesh faces",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            planar_hypotheses.len(),
            multi_face_count,
            single_face_count,
            covered_faces,
            mesh.faces.len(),
        );
        if config.verbose {
            for (i, h) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
                if h.faces.len() > 1 {
                    eprintln!(
                        "  Plane {}: {} faces, {} vertices, normal=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}], d={:.4}, err_max={:.2e}, err_min={:.2e}",
                        i, h.faces.len(), h.vertices.len(),
                        h.normal[0], h.normal[1], h.normal[2], h.distance,
```

```
fn select_surfaces(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    planar_hypotheses: &mut [PlanarHypothesis],
    cylindrical_hypotheses: &mut [CylindricalHypothesis],
    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
    viz: Option<&VizSender>,
) -> Vec<SelectedSurface> {
    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();

    // Step 1: Compute geometric area of each mesh face.
    let face_areas: Vec<f64> = (0..num_faces)
        .map(|fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    // Step 2: Build candidate list from multi-face hypotheses.
    struct Candidate {
        surface_type: u8, // 0=planar, 1=cylindrical, 2=spherical
        hypothesis_index: usize,
        faces: Vec<usize>,
    }

    let mut candidates: Vec<Candidate> = Vec::new();

    for (i, hyp) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        if hyp.faces.len() > 1 {
            candidates.push(Candidate {
                surface_type: 0,
                hypothesis_index: i,
                faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
    for (i, hyp) in cylindrical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        candidates.push(Candidate {
            surface_type: 1,
            hypothesis_index: i,
            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
        });
    }
    for (i, hyp) in spherical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        candidates.push(Candidate {
            surface_type: 2,
            hypothesis_index: i,
            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
        });
    }

    // Step 3: Greedy loop — select candidate with largest remaining area.
    let mut assigned = vec![false; num_faces];
    let mut selected: Vec<SelectedSurface> = Vec::new();

    loop {
        let mut best_ci = None;
        let mut best_area = 0.0_f64;

        for (ci, cand) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
            let area: f64 = cand.faces.iter()
                .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
                .map(|fi| face_areas[*fi])
                .sum();
            if area > best_area {
                best_area = area;
                best_ci = Some(ci);
            }
        }

        let Some(ci) = best_ci else { break };

        // Collect still-unassigned faces for the winning candidate.
        let sel_faces: Vec<usize> = candidates[ci].faces.iter()
            .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
            .copied()
            .collect();


        // Viz: show selected hypothesis and competing hypotheses
        if let Some(viz_sender) = viz {
            let mut overlay = viz::VizOverlay::new();

            // Already-assigned faces in dark gray
            let assigned_faces: Vec<usize> = (0..num_faces)
                .filter(|&fi| assigned[fi])
                .collect();
            if !assigned_faces.is_empty() {
                overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                    face_indices: assigned_faces,
                    color: [0.3, 0.3, 0.3, 1.0],
                });
            }

            // Faces being assigned in green
            overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                face_indices: sel_faces.clone(),
                color: [0.0, 0.8, 0.0, 1.0],
            });

            // Find competing hypotheses that involve any of the sel_faces
            let sel_face_set: HashSet<usize> = sel_faces.iter().copied().collect();
            for (oi, other) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
                if oi == ci {
                    continue;
                }
                let competing_faces: Vec<usize> = other.faces.iter()
                    .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi] && sel_face_set.contains(fi))
                    .copied()
                    .collect();
                if !competing_faces.is_empty() {
                    overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                        face_indices: competing_faces,
                        color: [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
                    });
                    // Show the competing hypothesis surface overlay
```

```
            for &fi in &sel_faces {
                let f = &mesh.faces[fi];

                }
            }
            let mut v: Vec<usize> = vset.into_iter().collect();
            v.sort_unstable();
            v
        };

        // Update hypothesis face/vertex lists to reflect only the assigned faces,
        // and create the SelectedSurface entry.
        let selected_surface = match candidates[ci].surface_type {
            0 => {
                let idx = candidates[ci].hypothesis_index;
                planar_hypotheses[idx].faces = sel_faces;
                planar_hypotheses[idx].vertices = sel_vertices;
                SelectedSurface::Planar(idx)
            }
            1 => {
                let idx = candidates[ci].hypothesis_index;
                cylindrical_hypotheses[idx].faces = sel_faces;
                cylindrical_hypotheses[idx].vertices = sel_vertices;
                SelectedSurface::Cylindrical(idx)
            }
            _ => {
                let idx = candidates[ci].hypothesis_index;
                spherical_hypotheses[idx].faces = sel_faces;
                spherical_hypotheses[idx].vertices = sel_vertices;
                SelectedSurface::Spherical(idx)
            }
        };
        selected.push(selected_surface);
    }

    // Step 4: Assign remaining faces to their single-face planar hypothesis.
    for (fi, assigned) in assigned.iter().enumerate().take(num_faces) {
        if !assigned {
            let pi = mesh.faces[fi].planar_hypothesis;
            assert!(pi >= 0, "face {fi} has no hypothesis assigned");
            selected.push(SelectedSurface::Planar(pi as usize));
        }
    }

    selected
}
```

```
//! Stage 2.2: Cylindrical hypothesis deduction and comparison.

use std::collections::{HashSet, VecDeque};

use levenberg_marquardt::{LeastSquaresProblem, LevenbergMarquardt};
use nalgebra::{Const, Dyn, OMatrix, OVector, Owned};
use opencascade_sys::gp;

use crate::config::Config;
use crate::stage1::{self, ConnectedMesh, MeshFace, MeshVertex, UNDEDUCED_CYLINDRICAL_HYPOTHESIS, NO_HYPOTHESIS};
use crate::viz::{self, VizAction, VizSender};

use super::{
    build_normal_covariance, centered_cylinder_overlay, cross3, dot3, face_area, face_centroid,
    normalize3, perpendicular_basis, smallest_eigenvector_3x3, viz_custom, viz_face_centroid,
    viz_face_normal, CylindricalHypothesis, Stage2CompareError, MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD,
    MIN_CYLINDER_FACES, REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER,
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Cylindrical fitting helpers
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Compute the distance from a vertex to a cylinder surface.
/// Returns the signed distance: positive if outside the cylinder, negative if inside.
pub(super) fn vertex_to_cylinder_distance(
    v: &MeshVertex,
    axis_origin: &[f64; 3],
    axis_direction: &[f64; 3],
    radius: f64,
) -> f64 {
    // Vector from axis origin to vertex
    let dx = v.x - axis_origin[0];
    let dy = v.y - axis_origin[1];
    let dz = v.z - axis_origin[2];
    // Project onto axis
    let t = dx * axis_direction[0] + dy * axis_direction[1] + dz * axis_direction[2];
    // Radial vector (perpendicular to axis)
    let rx = dx - t * axis_direction[0];
    let ry = dy - t * axis_direction[1];
    let rz = dz - t * axis_direction[2];
    let radial_dist = (rx * rx + ry * ry + rz * rz).sqrt();
    radial_dist - radius
}

/// Given an axis direction, project vertices onto the perpendicular plane
/// and fit a 2D circle. Returns (axis_origin_3d, radius) or None if degenerate.
fn fit_circle_for_axis(
    axis_dir: &[f64; 3],
    vertex_set: &HashSet<usize>,
    vertices: &[MeshVertex],
) -> Option<([f64; 3], f64)> {
    let (u, w) = perpendicular_basis(axis_dir);
    let verts: Vec<usize> = vertex_set.iter().copied().collect();
    let n = verts.len();
    if n < 3 {
        return None;
    }

    let mut xs = Vec::with_capacity(n);
    let mut ys = Vec::with_capacity(n);
    for &vi in &verts {
        let v = &vertices[vi];
        xs.push(v.x * u[0] + v.y * u[1] + v.z * u[2]);
        ys.push(v.x * w[0] + v.y * w[1] + v.z * w[2]);
    }

    // Center the 2D data to improve numerical conditioning.
    let nf = n as f64;
    let mean_x = xs.iter().sum::<f64>() / nf;
    let mean_y = ys.iter().sum::<f64>() / nf;
    for i in 0..n {
        xs[i] -= mean_x;
        ys[i] -= mean_y;
    }

    // Algebraic circle fit: x² + y² + Dx + Ey + F = 0
    let mut sx = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sy = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sx2 = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sy2 = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sxy = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sx3 = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sy3 = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sx2y = 0.0_f64;
    let mut sxy2 = 0.0_f64;

    for i in 0..n {
        let x = xs[i];
        let y = ys[i];
        sx += x;
        sy += y;
        sx2 += x * x;
        sy2 += y * y;
        sxy += x * y;
        sx3 += x * x * x;
        sy3 += y * y * y;
        sx2y += x * x * y;
        sxy2 += x * y * y;
    }
```

```
//! Stage 2: Surface Fitting
//!
//! 2.1: Deduce planar hypotheses
//! 2.2: Deduce cylindrical hypotheses
//! 2.3: Deduce spherical hypotheses
//! 2.4: Deduce ruled surface hypotheses
//! 2.5: Deduce NURBS hypotheses
//! 2.6: Select surfaces for reconstruction

mod cylindrical;
mod planar;
mod spherical;

use crate::config::Config;
use crate::stage1::{self, ConnectedMesh, MeshFace, MeshVertex};
use crate::viz::{self, VizAction, VizSender};
use opencascade_sys::gp;
use std::collections::HashSet;
use std::time::Instant;
use std::error::Error;
use std::fmt::{Display, Formatter};

use self::cylindrical::{
    compare_cylindrical_hypotheses, deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses, vertex_to_cylinder_distance,
};
use self::planar::{compare_planar_hypotheses, deduce_planar_hypotheses};
use self::spherical::{
    bounding_box_diagonal, compare_spherical_hypotheses, deduce_spherical_hypotheses,
    vertex_to_sphere_distance,
};

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Constants
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// Lower bound on cross product magnitude |n₁ × n₂| for a valid cylinder/sphere
/// seed pair. Since |n₁ × n₂| = sin(θ) for unit normals, this corresponds to a
/// minimum dihedral angle of arcsin(0.01) ≈ 0.57° (~0.6°). Seed pairs closer
/// than this are too nearly parallel for numerically stable axis estimation
/// (cylinders) or sphere fitting (spheres). Together with `angular_tol` (the
/// upper bound, typically 17.5°), this defines the valid seed-pair window
/// [~0.6°, angular_tol].
pub(crate) const MIN_CROSS_THRESHOLD: f64 = 0.01;

/// Fast-reject multiplier for BFS vertex-distance checks. When a candidate face
/// has a vertex farther than `REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER × vertex_tol` from the
/// current fitted surface, the distance is too large for a re-fit to absorb and
/// the face is rejected immediately without attempting re-fitting.
pub(crate) const REFIT_SKIP_MULTIPLIER: f64 = 2.0;

/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a cylindrical hypothesis.
/// Any real CAD tessellation of a cylinder produces at least 3 facets around
/// the circumference; this rejects spurious 2-face fits (e.g. adjacent torus
/// facets that locally approximate a cylinder).
pub(crate) const MIN_CYLINDER_FACES: usize = 3;

/// Minimum number of mesh faces required to accept a spherical hypothesis.
/// Sphere fitting has 4 degrees of freedom (cx, cy, cz, r), so at least 4
/// non-degenerate faces are needed for a well-determined fit. This also rejects
/// spurious fits from small patches that are consistent with many surface types.
pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_FACES: usize = 4;

/// Maximum sphere radius as a multiple of the mesh bounding-box diagonal.
/// With solid-angle coverage validation and surface-tolerance validation during
/// BFS, this no longer needs to be tight — those checks prevent pathological
/// growth. A large value serves as a numerical guardrail against degenerate fits.
pub(crate) const MAX_SPHERE_RADIUS_FACTOR: f64 = 1000.0;

/// Minimum eigenvalue ratio for solid-angle coverage validation.
/// Centroid-to-center direction vectors of a genuine spherical hypothesis span
/// 3D, so all eigenvalues of their covariance matrix are substantial.
/// Fillet-strip growth produces nearly coplanar directions (λ₃ ≈ 0).
pub(crate) const MIN_SPHERE_EIGENVALUE_RATIO: f64 = 0.01;
```
```

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Hypothesis data structures
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// A planar surface hypothesis fitted to a set of mesh faces.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PlanarHypothesis {
    /// Unit normal vector pointing outward from the shell/solid.
    pub normal: [f64; 3],
    /// Signed distance from origin to plane along the normal.
    pub distance: f64,
    /// Set of mesh face indices that fit this hypothesis.
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
    /// Set of mesh vertex indices on this plane.
    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
    /// Maximum (positive) distance from any vertex to the plane.
    pub error_max: f64,
    /// Minimum (most negative) distance from any vertex to the plane.
    pub error_min: f64,
    /// Sum of absolute vertex-to-plane distances.
    pub error_abs_sum: f64,
}

/// A cylindrical surface hypothesis fitted to a set of mesh faces.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct CylindricalHypothesis {
    /// A point on the cylinder axis.
    pub axis_origin: [f64; 3],
    /// Unit direction vector along the cylinder axis.
    pub axis_direction: [f64; 3],
    /// Radius of the cylinder (always positive).
    pub radius: f64,
    /// Whether the surface normal points away from the axis (convex=true) or toward it (concave=false).
    pub convex: bool,
    /// Set of mesh face indices that fit this hypothesis.
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
    /// Set of mesh vertex indices on this cylinder.
    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
    /// Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the cylinder surface.
    pub error_max: f64,
    /// Maximum absolute distance from any face centroid to the cylinder surface.
    pub centroid_error_max: f64,
    /// Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
    pub error_abs_sum: f64,
}

/// A spherical surface hypothesis fitted to a set of mesh faces.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct SphericalHypothesis {
    /// Center of the sphere.
    pub center: [f64; 3],
    /// Radius of the sphere (always positive).
    pub radius: f64,
    /// Whether the surface normal points away from center (convex) or toward it (concave).
    pub convex: bool,
    /// Set of mesh face indices that fit this hypothesis.
    pub faces: Vec<usize>,
    /// Set of mesh vertex indices on this sphere.
    pub vertices: Vec<usize>,
    /// Maximum absolute distance from any vertex to the sphere surface.
    pub error_max: f64,
    /// Maximum absolute distance from any face centroid to the sphere surface.
    pub centroid_error_max: f64,
    /// Sum of absolute vertex-to-surface distances.
    pub error_abs_sum: f64,
}

// TODO: Stage 2.4 - Ruled surface hypothesis
// TODO: Stage 2.5 - NURBS hypothesis

/// Identifies a selected surface hypothesis by type and index.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub enum SelectedSurface {
    Planar(usize),
    Cylindrical(usize),
    Spherical(usize),
    // TODO: RuledSurface(usize),
    // TODO: Nurbs(usize),
}

// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Stage 2 output
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/// The output of Stage 2: the mesh with all hypotheses populated and surfaces selected.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct Stage2Output {
    /// The mesh from stage 1 with per-face hypothesis indices populated.
    pub mesh: ConnectedMesh,
    /// All planar hypotheses deduced in stage 2.1.
    pub planar_hypotheses: Vec<PlanarHypothesis>,
    /// All cylindrical hypotheses deduced in stage 2.2.
    pub cylindrical_hypotheses: Vec<CylindricalHypothesis>,
    /// All spherical hypotheses deduced in stage 2.3.
    pub spherical_hypotheses: Vec<SphericalHypothesis>,
    /// Surfaces selected in stage 2.6 for reconstruction. Each face should be
    /// covered by exactly one selected surface.
    pub selected_surfaces: Vec<SelectedSurface>,
}
```

```
fn select_surfaces(
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    planar_hypotheses: &mut [PlanarHypothesis],
    cylindrical_hypotheses: &mut [CylindricalHypothesis],
    spherical_hypotheses: &mut [SphericalHypothesis],
    viz: Option<&VizSender>,
) -> Vec<SelectedSurface> {
    let num_faces = mesh.faces.len();

    // Step 1: Compute geometric area of each mesh face.
    let face_areas: Vec<f64> = (0..num_faces)
        .map(|fi| face_area(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices))
        .collect();

    // Step 2: Build candidate list from multi-face hypotheses.
    struct Candidate {
        surface_type: u8, // 0=planar, 1=cylindrical, 2=spherical
        hypothesis_index: usize,
        faces: Vec<usize>,
    }

    let mut candidates: Vec<Candidate> = Vec::new();

    for (i, hyp) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        if hyp.faces.len() > 1 {
            candidates.push(Candidate {
                surface_type: 0,
                hypothesis_index: i,
                faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
            });
        }
    }
    for (i, hyp) in cylindrical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        candidates.push(Candidate {
            surface_type: 1,
            hypothesis_index: i,
            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
        });
    }
    for (i, hyp) in spherical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
        candidates.push(Candidate {
            surface_type: 2,
            hypothesis_index: i,
            faces: hyp.faces.clone(),
        });
    }

    // Step 3: Greedy loop — select candidate with largest remaining area.
    let mut assigned = vec![false; num_faces];
    let mut selected: Vec<SelectedSurface> = Vec::new();

    loop {
        let mut best_ci = None;
        let mut best_area = 0.0_f64;

        for (ci, cand) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
            let area: f64 = cand.faces.iter()
                .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
                .map(|fi| face_areas[*fi])
                .sum();
            if area > best_area {
                best_area = area;
                best_ci = Some(ci);
            }
        }

        let Some(ci) = best_ci else { break };

        // Collect still-unassigned faces for the winning candidate.
        let sel_faces: Vec<usize> = candidates[ci].faces.iter()
            .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi])
            .copied()
            .collect();


        // Viz: show selected hypothesis and competing hypotheses
        if let Some(viz_sender) = viz {
            let mut overlay = viz::VizOverlay::new();

            // Already-assigned faces in dark gray
            let assigned_faces: Vec<usize> = (0..num_faces)
                .filter(|&fi| assigned[fi])
                .collect();
            if !assigned_faces.is_empty() {
                overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                    face_indices: assigned_faces,
                    color: [0.3, 0.3, 0.3, 1.0],
                });
            }

            // Faces being assigned in green
            overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                face_indices: sel_faces.clone(),
                color: [0.0, 0.8, 0.0, 1.0],
            });

            // Find competing hypotheses that involve any of the sel_faces
            let sel_face_set: HashSet<usize> = sel_faces.iter().copied().collect();
            for (oi, other) in candidates.iter().enumerate() {
                if oi == ci {
                    continue;
                }
                let competing_faces: Vec<usize> = other.faces.iter()
                    .filter(|fi| !assigned[**fi] && sel_face_set.contains(fi))
                    .copied()
                    .collect();
                if !competing_faces.is_empty() {
                    overlay.face_highlights.push(viz::FaceHighlight {
                        face_indices: competing_faces,
                        color: [0.5, 0.5, 0.5, 0.25],
                    });
                    // Show the competing hypothesis surface overlay
```

```
/// Validate selected surfaces against a reference STEP file.
fn compare_selected_surfaces(
    selected_surfaces: &[SelectedSurface],
    planar_hypotheses: &[PlanarHypothesis],
    cylindrical_hypotheses: &[CylindricalHypothesis],
    spherical_hypotheses: &[SphericalHypothesis],
    mesh: &ConnectedMesh,
    config: &Config,
) -> Result<(), Stage2CompareError> {
    let compare_shape = config.compare_shape.as_ref().unwrap();

    for (si, surface) in selected_surfaces.iter().enumerate() {
        let mut max_dist = 0.0_f64;

        let (hyp_type, face_indices) = match surface {
            SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => ("planar", &planar_hypotheses[*i].faces),
            SelectedSurface::Cylindrical(i) => ("cylindrical", &cylindrical_hypotheses[*i].faces),
            SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => ("spherical", &spherical_hypotheses[*i].faces),
        };

        for &fi in face_indices {
            let centroid = face_centroid(&mesh.faces[fi], &mesh.vertices);

            let projected = match surface {
                SelectedSurface::Planar(i) => {
                    let hyp = &planar_hypotheses[*i];
                    let dist_to_plane = hyp.normal[0] * centroid[0]
                        + hyp.normal[1] * centroid[1]
                        + hyp.normal[2] * centroid[2]
                        - hyp.distance;
                    [
                        centroid[0] - dist_to_plane * hyp.normal[0],
                        centroid[1] - dist_to_plane * hyp.normal[1],
                        centroid[2] - dist_to_plane * hyp.normal[2],
                    ]
                }
                SelectedSurface::Cylindrical(i) => {
                    let hyp = &cylindrical_hypotheses[*i];
                    let d = [
                        centroid[0] - hyp.axis_origin[0],
                        centroid[1] - hyp.axis_origin[1],
                        centroid[2] - hyp.axis_origin[2],
                    ];
                    let t = dot3(&d, &hyp.axis_direction);
                    let radial = [
                        d[0] - t * hyp.axis_direction[0],
                        d[1] - t * hyp.axis_direction[1],
                        d[2] - t * hyp.axis_direction[2],
                    ];
                    let radial_dist = (radial[0] * radial[0]
                        + radial[1] * radial[1]
                        + radial[2] * radial[2])
                        .sqrt();
                    if radial_dist > 1e-15 {
                        let scale = hyp.radius / radial_dist;
                        [
                            hyp.axis_origin[0] + t * hyp.axis_direction[0] + radial[0] * scale,
                            hyp.axis_origin[1] + t * hyp.axis_direction[1] + radial[1] * scale,
                            hyp.axis_origin[2] + t * hyp.axis_direction[2] + radial[2] * scale,
                        ]
                    } else {
                        centroid
                    }
                }
                SelectedSurface::Spherical(i) => {
                    let hyp = &spherical_hypotheses[*i];
                    let d = [
                        centroid[0] - hyp.center[0],
                        centroid[1] - hyp.center[1],
                        centroid[2] - hyp.center[2],
                    ];
                    let dist_to_center =
                        (d[0] * d[0] + d[1] * d[1] + d[2] * d[2]).sqrt();
                    if dist_to_center > 1e-15 {
                        let scale = hyp.radius / dist_to_center;
                        [
                            hyp.center[0] + d[0] * scale,
                            hyp.center[1] + d[1] * scale,
                            hyp.center[2] + d[2] * scale,
                        ]
                    } else {
                        centroid
                    }
                }
            };

            let pt = gp::Pnt::new_real3(projected[0], projected[1], projected[2]);
            let dist = stage1::min_di
```

```
pub fn stage2(config: &Config, mut mesh: ConnectedMesh, viz: Option<&crate::viz::VizSender>) -> Result<Stage2Output, Stage2Error> {
    // Stage 2.1: Deduce planar hypotheses
    let t = Instant::now();
    let viz_21 = if config.viz_active(2, 1) { viz } else { None };
    let (mut planar_hypotheses, planar_quit) = deduce_planar_hypotheses(&mut mesh, config.vertex_tolerance_mm, config.verbosity, viz_21);

    if !config.quiet {
        let multi_face_count = planar_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| h.faces.len() > 1).count();
        let single_face_count = planar_hypotheses.len() - multi_face_count;
        let covered_faces: usize = planar_hypotheses.iter().map(|h| h.faces.len()).sum();
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.1 ({:.3}s): Deduced {} planar hypotheses ({} multi-face, {} single-face) covering {} of {} mesh faces",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            planar_hypotheses.len(),
            multi_face_count,
            single_face_count,
            covered_faces,
            mesh.faces.len(),
        );
        if config.verbose {
            for (i, h) in planar_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
                if h.faces.len() > 1 {
                    eprintln!(
                        "  Plane {}: {} faces, {} vertices, normal=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}], d={:.4}, err_max={:.2e}, err_min={:.2e}",
                        i, h.faces.len(), h.vertices.len(),
                        h.normal[0], h.normal[1], h.normal[2], h.distance,
                        h.error_max, h.error_min,
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }

    // Compare against STEP file if --compare was specified
    if config.compare_shape.is_some() {
        compare_planar_hypotheses(&planar_hypotheses, &mesh, config)?;
        if !config.quiet {
            eprintln!("  Compare: all planar hypothesis centroids within tolerance");
        }
    }

    if planar_quit {
        return Ok(Stage2Output {
            mesh,
            planar_hypotheses,
            cylindrical_hypotheses: Vec::new(),
            spherical_hypotheses: Vec::new(),
            selected_surfaces: Vec::new(),
        });
    }



    if !config.stage.at_least(2, 2) {
        return Ok(Stage2Output {
            mesh,
            planar_hypotheses,
            cylindrical_hypotheses: Vec::new(),
            spherical_hypotheses: Vec::new(),
            selected_surfaces: Vec::new(),
        });
    }

    // Stage 2.2: Deduce cylindrical hypotheses
    let t = Instant::now();
    let viz_22 = if config.viz_active(2, 2) { viz } else { None };
    let (mut cylindrical_hypotheses, cylindrical_quit) = deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses(
        &mut mesh, config.vertex_tolerance_mm,
        config.surface_tolerance_mm, config.angular_tolerance_rad,
        config.verbosity,
        viz_22,
    );

    if !config.quiet {
        let covered_faces: usize = cylindrical_hypotheses.iter().map(|h| h.faces.len()).sum();
        let convex_count = cylindrical_hypotheses.iter().filter(|h| h.convex).count();
        let concave_count = cylindrical_hypotheses.len() - convex_count;
        eprintln!(
            "Stage 2.2 ({:.3}s): Deduced {} cylindrical hypotheses ({} convex, {} concave) covering {} faces",
            t.elapsed().as_secs_f64(),
            cylindrical_hypotheses.len(),
            convex_count,
            concave_count,
            covered_faces,
        );
        if config.verbose {
            for (i, h) in cylindrical_hypotheses.iter().enumerate() {
                eprintln!(
                    "  Cylinder {}: {} faces, {} vertices, r={:.4}, {}, \
axis_origin=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}], axis_dir=[{:.4}, {:.4}, {:.4}], \
vtx_err_max={:.2e}, cen_err_max={:.2e}",

                    if h.convex { "convex" } else { "concave" },
                    h.axis_origin[0], h.axis_origin[1], h.axis_origin[2],
```

I see — the seeding checks  but doesn't check `cylindrical_hypothesis` or `spherical_hypothesis`. This is the same pattern as the cylindrical module — it checks its own hypothesis field but doesn't exclude faces already claimed by other surface types. That's correct because the surface selection (stage 2.6) is what resolves overlapping claims. `angular_tolerance` defaults to 17.5°. In , the condition is , meaning two faces must have normals within 17.5° to qualify as a seed pair.

For a 20-segment cone, the azimuthal angle between adjacent strip normals is 360/20 = 18°. So the angle between adjacent normals is exactly 18° which exceeds the 17.5° threshold! That's why no pairs qualify.

But wait — the seeding also tries  which is a neighbor of  but not of . So  could be 2 strips away, with a 36° normal difference from . That would also fail.

The issue is the  is also used for the  pair check, not . Let me re-read: the function uses  for direct neighbors and  for the second hop. So for the cone, if fi and n1 are azimuthally adjacent (18°), they FAIL the 17.5° test.

However, vertically adjacent faces on the cone (same strip, different height) would have the SAME normal direction. So  → passes. But then the  check would reject them since the cross product of identical normals is zero!

This is the fundamental problem: on a cone, adjacent faces in the same strip have the same normal (cross=0, rejected), and adjacent faces in different strips have normals 18° apart (> 17.5°, rejected on coarse meshes).

The  function is designed for cylinders where adjacent faces have different normals. For cones, the seeding strategy needs to be different. On a cone, the key identifying feature is that faces in the same strip have the same normal but different positions.

The proper fix is to relax the  for conical seeding. For a cone, what we want is:
1. At least some of the 3 seed faces should NOT be coplanar (to distinguish from a plane)
2. At least some should NOT have the same normal (to get axis information)

Instead of the very restrictive per-pair qualification, I should use a triple qualification: the 3 faces together should have enough normal diversity (at least 2 different normals).

Let me modify the seeding to allow coplanar pairs and pairs with the same normal, but require the overall triple to have normal diversity:

I now understand the problem clearly. The seeding strategy requires  which demands normals be within  (17.5°) AND have a cross product magnitude > 0.01. For cones, adjacent faces in the same strip have identical normals (cross=0, fails), and adjacent faces in different strips have normals ~18° apart (fails angular tolerance on coarse meshes).

The fix is to change the seeding to not require  for each pair, but instead use a looser triple qualification: the 3 seed faces must have at least 2 distinct normal directions (cross > threshold), and the triple must collectively have some normal spread to allow cone fitting.

This is because  finds the **smallest** eigenvector. For a cone, the normals are distributed in a cone around the axis. The **mean** normal direction is along the axis for a full-revolution cone. After subtracting the mean, the covariance should have the smallest eigenvalue along the axis BUT — with only 3 faces from the same neighborhood, the eigenvector isn't well-determined.

This is a fundamental problem: 3 neighboring cone faces don't have enough normal diversity to correctly determine the cone axis. The covariance approach only works when faces span a significant arc.

The real root problem is the axis estimation. For 3 neighboring faces, the cross-product approach should work when 2 of the 3 normals are distinct. Let me think about the geometry more carefully.

A 20-segment cone has 20 strips around. Each strip has multiple faces. The faces within a strip have the same normal direction (they're coplanar). Adjacent strips have normals that differ by 360°/20 = 18°.

For seed triple (fi, n1, n2) where n1 is neighbor of fi and n2 is neighbor of n1:
- If all 3 are in the same strip: only 1 normal → can't determine axis
- If fi and n1 are in same strip, n2 in adjacent strip: 2 normals → d1 = n1-n2, axis = d1 × avg(n1,n2)

With only 2 distinct normals differentiated by 18°, the difference vector  has magnitude `2*sin(9°) ≈ 0.31`. The cross product  would need the avg to not be parallel to d1.

Let me check: cones normals make angle θ with the radial direction. For two adjacent strips, their normals differ by rotation of 18° around the cone axis. Both normals have equal projection onto the axis (±sin(θ)). The difference  is perpendicular to the axis. The average  has both a component along the axis and in the radial direction (bisecting them). So  should give the axis component.

But  might be small if the radial component and d1 are nearly aligned, which they would be for small angular differences. Let me check a specific example: for a cone with half-angle ~36.9° (15mm radius, 20mm height → tan(θ)=15/20=0.75, θ≈36.9°):

Normal at angle φ around axis:  (axis along z, opening downward).

For two adjacent strips: φ₁=0°, φ₂=18°:
- n1 = [cos(θ), 0, -sin(θ)] = [0.8, 0, -0.6]
- n2 = [cos(18°)·cos(θ), sin(18°)·cos(θ), -sin(θ)] = [0.951*0.8, 0.309*0.8, -0.6] = [0.761, 0.247, -0.6]
- d1 = n1-n2 = [0.039, -0.247, 0]
- avg = n1+n2 = [1.561, 0.247, -1.2]
- d1×avg = [(-0.247)(-1.2) - 0*0.247, 0*1.561 - 0.039*(-1.2), 0.039*0.247 - (-0.247)*1.561]
  = [0.296, 0.047, 0.010 + 0.386] = [0.296, 0.047, 0.396]

After normalization: [0.596, 0.094, 0.797] — this is close to [0,0,1] but not great. The actual axis is [0,0,1]. The z-component dominates but there's significant x-contamination.

Actually:  = cross of a horizontal vector and a mixed vector. The result should have its largest component along z (the axis). Let me recalculate more carefully.

d1 = [0.039, -0.247, 0]
avg = [1.561, 0.247, -1.2]

cross:
x: (-0.247)*(-1.2) - 0*(0.247) = 0.2964
y: 0*(1.561) - (0.039)*(-1.2) = 0.0468  
z: (0.039)*(0.247) - (-0.247)*(1.561) = 0.00963 + 0.3856 = 0.3952

Magnitude: sqrt(0.2964² + 0.0468² + 0.3952²) = sqrt(0.0879 + 0.00219 + 0.1562) = sqrt(0.2463) = 0.496

Normalized: [0.597, 0.0943, 0.796]

This is off by about 37° from the true axis [0,0,1]. Not great as an initial estimate. The LM optimization should correct it, but it needs reasonable starting point.

A better approach: use the **covariance of normals** even with few faces, but also try the **cross-product of two normals directly**. For a cone, two normals n1, n2 that are on the cone surface:  gives a vector perpendicular to both, which is tangent to the cone base circle. The axis is perpendicular to this tangent and in the plane of the normals.

But these 4-5 base vertices are clustered in one area of the base circle (near the seed faces), so their centroid is NOT at the cone center. The direction from apex to this local centroid doesn't align with the real axis.

This is a fundamental geometric problem: **3 adjacent cone faces near the apex don't provide enough information to determine the cone axis direction**, because the base vertices are all on one side.

Let me reconsider the approach. For cylinders, the code probably works because cylinder normals all lie in a plane perpendicular to the axis, so even 2-3 faces give a good axis direction. For cones, the normals have a varying component along the axis.

**Better approach for cone detection**: Instead of trying to fit a full cone from 3 faces, use a two-phase approach:
1. Phase 1: Build a larger initial seed by using a "normal-similarity BFS" (just gather faces whose normals smoothly transition, similar to how cylinder seeding works but without fitting)
2. Phase 2: Once we have enough faces (say 8+), use the covariance approach which is robust

But this would be a bigger refactor. Let me try something simpler first: **relax the BFS expansion to tolerate larger initial errors**, so even with a bad initial fit, the BFS can grab more faces, then refit gives a better result.

The vertex tolerance is the bottleneck. Worst distances of 0.8-3.1mm against 0.4mm tolerance. The profile fit from 3 faces with a bad initial axis gives cone parameters that don't fit the vertices well.

The issue is that  Strategy 2 (shared vertex) gives the axis as apex→base_centroid, but the base centroid from only 4-5 vertices is off-center. This produces a tilted axis, and the profile fit then gives wrong half-angle and apex.

For the (0,57,56) seed with worst_dist=0.8mm — it's close to passing the 0.4mm threshold. The tolerance is the  from config (0.4mm). I could relax the seed tolerance, but first let me fix the axis estimation.

**Key insight**: The problem is that with 3 adjacent faces at the cone apex, the 4-5 base vertices are clustered in a 2-strip arc (~36° of the base circle). The centroid of these clustered base vertices doesn't align with the cone's actual center. Strategy 2 gives a tilted axis.

**Better approach for Strategy 2**: Instead of computing centroid of base vertices, compute the center of the circle they lie on. For vertices on the base circle, we can fit a circle or at least use the fact that they're equidistant from the apex.

Actually, wait. For a cone, each base vertex is at equal distance from the apex (they're all on a circle on a sphere centered at apex). So the apex→base_centroid direction IS the axis only when the base vertices span a full circle. For a partial arc, the centroid is biased.

**Actually the simplest fix**: use a wider tolerance for the initial 3-face seed validation (since we know the fit is rough), and let the BFS + refit improve it. Currently uses  (0.4mm). For initial seeds, use e.g., 5× that:

The problem is clear: the half_angle is ~2.2° instead of the true ~20.5°. The axis estimation produces a wrong result when only 3 faces are used. Let me look deeper at what's happening — the profile fit is severely wrong.

Let me trace the actual issue. The profiles with 3 adjacent faces are too close together and the axis is wrong, leading to garbage fits. I need to understand the mesh geometry better.

function which calls these:

Read [conical.rs](../src/stage2/conical.rs#400-400), lines 400 to 560

Let me now look at what the key problem is. The debug output shows the fitted half_angle is ~2.2° when it should be ~20.5°. The  Strategy 2 (shared vertex) is producing a tilted axis because the 3 adjacent seed faces are clustered, and from there the profile fit fails.

Let me think about this differently. The fundamental problem is:
1. With only 3 faces that share an apex vertex, the base vertices span a small arc
2. The centroid of those base vertices is biased, giving a tilted axis
3. The tilted axis makes the profile fit produce wrong half-angle

A better approach: instead of trying to get a perfect axis from 3 faces, use the fact that on a cone, face normals all make the same angle with the axis. So for 3 faces with normals n1, n2, n3, the axis is the vector that makes the same angle with all three normals. This is a well-defined geometric problem.

2. The vertices don't have enough height spread for profile fitting

The OCCT cone construction looks reasonable. The issue might be that OCCT's  doesn't accept ref_radius=0.0 or there's a different convention for the axis/apex.

Let me check OCCT docs: `Geom_ConicalSurface(gp_Ax3, Standard_Real Ang, Standard_Real Radius)` — the Radius is the radius at the reference plane (at origin). If origin is at apex, radius=0 is correct theoretically. But OCCT might not like a degenerate zero-radius cone.

Actually, looking at the OCCT docs more carefully: the Ax3 origin is NOT the apex. The Ax3 origin is on the axis, and the Radius is the radius at that point. The apex is at . So if we set origin=apex and radius=0, the apex IS at the origin, which should work.

But maybe OCCT requires radius > 0? Let me check if there's a validation issue. Or maybe the segfault is in stage 4, not stage 3.

Actually, the fundamental problem is that any set of faces from a sphere or cylinder CAN be approximately fitted as a cone with a distant apex. We need to filter out these false positives.

The best approach: add an **apex distance sanity check**. For a false cone fitted to a spherical/cylindrical region, the apex will be very far from the vertices (1000+ mm away). Real cones have apexes within a reasonable distance. Let me check the false positive parameters:

- cone 0: apex at [21, 191, 1257] — that's 1257mm away in z! False positive.
- cone 1: apex at [15, -1133, -154] — 1133mm away. False positive.
- cone 2: apex at [294, -102, 113] — hundreds of mm away. False positive.
- cone 4: apex at [14, -10, 20] — this one is close but ha=60.8° and err=0.4mm.

The real cone (cone_15x20_medium): apex at [-51, 0, 40] which is close to the mesh.

A good filter: **reject cones where the apex is more than some multiple of the maximum r (radius at base) from the mesh**. For a cone with half_angle θ, the apex is at distance r/sin(θ) from the base circle. If the half_angle is tiny (< 2°), the apex is extremely far and it's basically a cylinder.

The cleanest fix: **reject cones with half_angle < 2° or > 80°** (near-cylindrical or near-planar). And also reject cones where the vertex error is > surface_tol / 2 (tighter tolerance for cone acceptance):

Now I understand the full flow. The problem is clear: the apex-vertex seeding is finding vertices on spherical/cylindrical patches where many faces meet at a vertex with non-coplanar normals (eigenval/trace < 0.3). On a sphere, every vertex has non-coplanar incident face normals, and the "cone" fit can approximate the surface within tolerance.

```sh
cd /Users/sethml/src/brepper && git commit -F - <<'EOF'
Implement conical surface detection and fitting (stage 2.4)

Adds complete conical surface hypothesis support to the STL-to-STEP pipeline:

**Cone detection (src/stage2/conical.rs, new ~1200 lines):**
- Apex-vertex seeding: identifies vertices with many non-coplanar-normal
  incident faces (eigenval/trace < 0.3 from normal covariance), uses all
  incident faces as seed set for robust axis estimation
- Axis estimation from normal covariance smallest eigenvector, with
  orientation fix (flip axis when h_sum < 0 to ensure axis points apex→base)
- Profile fitting via linear regression in (h, r) coordinates to get
  half-angle and apex position
- Levenberg-Marquardt 6-parameter refinement (axis perturbation + apex + half-angle)
- BFS region growing with vertex+centroid distance checks, angular tolerance,
  convexity determination by majority vote after final re-fit
- False positive rejection: half-angle bounds (2°–85°), vertex error check,
  apex distance sanity (< 10× bounding box diagonal), normal-axis angle
  consistency (std_dev < angular_tol/2 rejects sphere patches fitted as cones)

**Structural changes:**
- ConicalHypothesis struct with apex, axis, half-angle, convexity, faces,
  vertices, error metrics
- MeshFace.conical_hypothesis field and UNDEDUCED_CONICAL_HYPOTHESIS constant
- SelectedSurface::Conical variant in stage 2.6 surface selection
- Stage 3 is_periodic check includes Conical (prevents segfault on full-circle edges)
- OCCT ConicalSurface creation with origin at apex, ref_radius=0

**Results:**
- cone_15x20_medium: 58 faces, ha=20.56°, err_max=2.4e-6mm
- simple_cone (ccad): 212 faces, ha=9.46°, volume diff 4.07e-9
- block_with_conical_hole: 131 concave faces, ha=5.19°, err_max=5.6e-7mm
- Zero false positives on rounded_cube (spheres/cylinders not misclassified)
- All 325 tests pass, zero clippy warnings
```

## Summary

Fixed false positive cone detection and completed the conical surface implementation. The key changes this session:

1. **False positive rejection filters** in `src/stage2/conical.rs`: Added four filters that reject spurious cones fitted to spherical/cylindrical patches:
   - Half-angle bounds (2°–85°) — rejects near-cylindrical and near-planar cones
   - Vertex error > surface_tol after final re-fit
   - Apex distance > 10× bounding box diagonal from mesh center
   - Normal-axis angle consistency — std_dev of  must be <  (the key filter that catches sphere patches fitted as ~56° cones)

2. **All 325 tests pass**, zero clippy warnings, zero false positives on rounded_cube model.

3. Updated `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md`, `LEARNINGS.md`, and `README.md`.

## Test Pattern Report for Cone Tests

### 1. Macro Definitions

**Stage 2.1 compare** (`test_stl_step_stage2_compare!`):
```rust
macro_rules! test_stl_step_stage2_compare {
    ($name:ident, $stl_path:literal, $step_path:literal) => {
        #[test]
        fn $name() {
            let stl = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $stl_path);
            let step = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $step_path);
            let config = config_for_compare(&stl, &step);
            run_stage2(&config);
        }
    };
}
```

**Stage 2.2 compare** (`test_stl_step_stage22_compare!`): Same pattern but uses `config_for_compare_stage22` and also calls `assert_step_surfaces_covered` for Plane + Cylinder types.

**Stage 2.3 compare** (`test_stl_step_stage23_compare!`): Same pattern, `config_for_compare_stage23`, checks Plane + Cylinder + Sphere.

**Stage 2.6 compare** (`test_stl_step_stage26_compare!`):
```rust
macro_rules! test_stl_step_stage26_compare {
    ($name:ident, $stl_path:literal, $step_path:literal) => {
        #[test]
        fn $name() {
            let stl = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $stl_path);
            let step = format!("{}/{}", manifest_dir(), $step_path);
            let config = config_for_compare_stage26(&stl, &step);
            run_stage2(&config);
        }
    };
}
```

**Cylinder param matching** (`test_cylinder_params_match!`): Uses `config_for_stl_stage22`, calls `assert_cylinders_match_step`.

**Sphere param matching** (`test_sphere_params_match!`): Uses `config_for_stl_stage23`, calls `assert_spheres_match_step`.

**Stage 3 tests**: No macros — each test is a hand-written `#[test] fn`. Pattern: `config_for_compare_34()` → `run_stage3_34()`.

**Stage 4 tests**: No macros — hand-written `#[test] fn`. Pattern: `config_for_compare(stl, output_path, step)` → `run_stage4()`. Note stage 4 takes 3 paths: STL input, STEP output (in `tmp/`), and reference STEP.

### 2. Naming Conventions

- `{source}_{model}_stage{NN}_compare` — e.g., `ccad_cube_stage22_compare`, `onshape_cone_stage26_compare`
- `{source}_{model}_params_match` — e.g., `ccad_simple_cylinder_params_match`
- Count/topology tests: descriptive names like `simple_cylinder_produces_one_cylindrical_hypothesis`, `cube_topology`
- Stage 3 face creation: `{model}_face_creation`
- Stage 4 output tests: `{model}_step_output`

### 3. Existing Cone-Related Tests

Only **two** cone tests exist, both in [tests/stage2_integration.rs](tests/stage2_integration.rs):

1. **`onshape_cone_stage2_compare`** (line 336) — stage 2.1 compare using `tests/onshape/cone_15x20_medium.stl` + `tests/onshape/cone_15x20.step`
2. **`onshape_cone_stage26_compare`** (line 1165) — stage 2.6 compare using the same files

There are **no** cone tests in stage3 or stage4 integration tests. No cone topology tests, no cone face creation tests, no cone param matching tests.

### 4. `ConicalHypothesis` struct fields

From [src/stage2/mod.rs](src/stage2/mod.rs#L153):
- `apex: [f64; 3]`
- `axis_direction: [f64; 3]`
- `half_angle: f64` (radians)
- `convex: bool`
- `faces: Vec<usize>`
- `vertices: Vec<usize>`
- `error_max: f64`
- `centroid_error_max: f64`
- `error_abs_sum: f64`

`SelectedSurface::Conical(usize)` already exists and is handled in the `all_faces_covered_by_selection` test.

### 5. Unit test patterns in `src/stage2/mod.rs`

The `#[cfg(test)] mod tests` (line 1344) uses:
- `load_stage1(stl_name)` helper to load + validate + fuse a mesh from a relative path like `"ccad/generated/simple_cylinder.stl"`
- Direct calls to `deduce_planar_hypotheses`, `deduce_cylindrical_hypotheses`, `deduce_spherical_hypotheses` with explicit tolerance parameters
- `build_mesh(verts, tris)` helper for synthetic point sets
- Tests already reference `UNDEDUCED_CONICAL_HYPOTHESIS` in `make_triangle_face`

### 7. Config Helper Pattern

Each stage level has its own `config_for_stl_stageNN` and `config_for_compare_stageNN` that set `--stage=N.M`. For cone tests at stage 2.4, you'd need:
```rust
fn config_for_stl_stage24(stl_path: &str) -> config::Config {
    config::parse_config_from(["brepper", stl_path, "--stage=2.4", "-q"]).unwrap()
}
fn config_for_compare_stage24(stl_path: &str, step_path: &str) -> config::Config {
    let mut config = config::parse_config_from(
        ["brepper", stl_path, "--compare", step_path, "--stage=2.4", "-q"]
    ).unwrap();
    config.load_compare_step().unwrap();
    config
}
```

And a `test_stl_step_stage24_compare!` macro following the same pattern, potentially calling `assert_step_surfaces_covered` with `Cone` added to the check types.

> 💭 The block_with_conical_hole fails at stage 3.1 because edge 10 (between cone face 2 and planar face 3) is 9.36mm from the nearest STEP edge. Let me understand the topology better.
> 
> Looking at the output:
> - Face 2 is the conical surface
> - Faces 3-12 are individual planar faces (each with 1 adjacent face - the cone)
> - These are the faces created by the cone intersecting the flat surfaces
> 
> The problem is that there are many small planar faces (faces 3-12) adjacent to the cone. In the STEP reference, the block_with_conical_hole should have 6 planar faces + 1 concave cone. But here, the cone surface is adjacent to 12 different faces (edges 10-19), each with ~10 boundary mesh vertices but no vertex endpoints (closed loops).
> 
> 
> The issue is that stage 2 is fragmenting the top and bottom faces into separate surfaces where the conical bore cuts through, rather than merging them back into the original 6 block faces. I'm getting 17 surfaces (16 planar fragments + 1 cone) when I should have just 7 (6 block faces + 1 cone).
> 
> The root cause is a topology mismatch: the tessellation creates many small coplanar patches around the conical bore that should be merged into single faces with holes, but instead they're being treated as separate surfaces. This is also causing edge mismatches at stage 3.1 - the STEP reference has just 2 circular edges where the cone meets the top and bottom, but the current model has 12 edges connecting the cone to 10 small planar faces. I'm noticing the planar surfaces detected in stage 2 don't have standard cube face normals—they all have a z-component of around -0.089, which suggests the plane detection algorithm might be picking up tilted or distorted surfaces rather than the actual flat faces of the block. The cone hypothesis accounts for 131 of those 229 faces, but the planar hypotheses don't quite add up either—some have more than 4 faces each. I'm realizing the block_with_conical_hole should be simpler: just a rectangular block with 6 planar faces and a single conical bore, so something's off with how the faces are being counted or grouped in the detection output.
> 
> Looking at the normals of those 16 planar surfaces more carefully, they're not aligned with the standard block face directions—they have slanted normals like [-0.3407, -0.9359, -0.0892], which suggests these aren't the main block faces at all but rather the fragmented surfaces created around the conical hole.
> 
> The real issue is that the cone's facets are being split across two different surface types: some are being classified as part of the cone hypothesis while others are being selected as separate planar surfaces in stage 2.6, which is fragmenting what should be a single conical surface.
> 
> Looking at the face counts, the mesh has roughly 229 total faces, with stage 2.6 selecting 16 planar surfaces plus 1 conical surface. For a block with a conical hole, the STEP reference should ideally have just 7 surfaces: top face (plane with hole), bottom face, 4 rectangular sides, and 1 cone. The tessellation creates triangles that fan from the circular hole outward, but since they're all on the same plane (like z=10 for the top), they should maintain consistent orientation rather than diverging.
> 
> However, examining the normal vectors reveals they all share a z-component of -0.0892 with x,y components rotating in equal angular steps around the z-axis—this pattern actually matches the conical bore surface facets, not the block faces. But wait, this is for cone detection - it doesn't check for planar/cylindrical/spherical hypotheses. The cone BFS simply looks at mesh adjacency and won't be blocked by other hypothesis types.
> 
> 
> There's also a check that faces must have normals computed. Looking at the cone tessellation more carefully, the conical bore is made up of strips of adjacent triangles that form facets lying on tangent planes to the cone surface. Since these facets are generated from a tessellation with discrete angular steps (like 18° increments around the base), adjacent facets have normals that differ by roughly that same angular amount.
> 
> But here's the puzzle: the planar hypotheses are finding groups of 4 faces each, and if there are 18 strips with 4 faces per strip, that's 72 faces from the cone alone. Yet the cone has 127 faces total, which doesn't quite add up—I'm getting 199 faces from the cone plus 30 from the box faces, but the mesh has 229 total, so something's off with my face count breakdown.
> 
> Let me trace back through the runs to see where the discrepancy is. The earlier stage reported 131 faces for the cone, but the verbose output shows 127. I need to check if these are from different runs or if there's a precision issue I'm missing.
> 
> Looking at the cone BFS algorithm itself, the issue seems to be in how it's expanding through neighboring faces. The BFS checks whether faces are already claimed by a conical hypothesis, then validates candidates using an angular tolerance check on the dihedral angle between adjacent faces, followed by distance and refitting checks. The angular threshold of 17.5° might be too strict and is rejecting valid cone facets that should be included.
> 
> For a tessellated cone with roughly 20 circumferential divisions like in block_with_conical_hole, adjacent strips have a dihedral angle around 18°, which exceeds the 17.5° tolerance. This causes the BFS to fail when trying to expand from one strip to the next, so the cone only captures faces within a single strip rather than growing around the entire circumference. So the dihedral angle between circumferentially adjacent facets on a cone is reduced by the cosine of the half-angle compared to a cylinder. For this specific cone with a 5.19° half-angle and 20 circumferential divisions, that works out to about 17.9°, which exceeds the 17.5° angular tolerance threshold and prevents growth between strips. The solution would involve either relaxing that tolerance or adjusting the tessellation parameters. angular tolerance slightly for cone BFS (but that might cause false positives)
> 2. Skip the angular tolerance check in cone BFS (since vertex distance is the true discriminator)
> 3. Be smarter about which dihedral angle to check (use the angle between the face normal and the cone's expected normal, rather than the inter-face dihedral)
> 
> Actually, looking at the code more carefully, the angular check compared the candidate face's normal with its already-claimed neighbor's normal. This is the proper dihedral angle between adjacent faces. For cylinder BFS, this same check works because cylindrical facets are always within angular_tol by design. But for cones, the tessellator creates facets that are close to but slightly exceed the angular tolerance.
> 
> The simplest fix would be to relax or remove the angular tolerance check for cone BFS entirely, since the cone vertex-distance check already constrains which faces belong and is a much better discriminator. Alternatively, I could check the angle between the candidate face's normal and the expected cone normal at that face's location instead of the inter-face dihedral—on a true cone, I'd compute the expected normal from the radial direction and half-angle, then verify the face normal matches that expectation within tolerance.
> 
> Actually, I think relaxing the angular check in cone BFS is the right move. Cylinders need it as a performance optimization to prevent BFS from jumping across sharp edges, but cones have the apex as a natural barrier that prevents jumping to perpendicular faces. The same logic applies to cylinders too—vertex distance should already prevent that. So the simplest fix is to remove or relax the angular tolerance check in cone BFS while keeping the vertex and centroid distance checks as the primary discriminators.

> I'm identifying the new learnings that need to be added from this session. The BFS angular tolerance issue came up because tessellated cones with N circumferential divisions create inter-strip dihedral angles around 360°/N—for N=20 that's roughly 18°, which exceeds the default 17.5° threshold. Doubling the angular tolerance for cone BFS works because vertex distance is the real discriminator. I also need to document the cone apex UV bounds fix, which appears to be cut off.

```sh
cd /Users/sethml/src/brepper && git add -A && git commit -m "Fix cone pipeline failures: BFS angular tolerance, apex UV bounds, concave flag

Three bugs prevented cone test models from passing the full pipeline:

1. Cone BFS angular tolerance (stage 2.4): tessellated cones with ~20
   circumferential divisions have ~18° inter-strip dihedral angles,
   exceeding the default 17.5° angular_tol. Use 2× angular_tol for
   cone BFS since vertex-distance is the primary discriminator.
   Fixes block_with_conical_hole capturing only 127/167 cone facets.

2. Cone apex UV bounds (stage 3.4): full (non-truncated) cones with a
   single boundary edge produced zero V-range, creating degenerate
   faces that failed BRepCheck. Extend V to 0 (apex) by sampling mesh
   centroids, analogous to sphere pole V-extension.
   Fixes onshape cone_15x20_medium sewing failure.

3. Cone concave flag (stage 3.4): is_concave match in create_periodic_face
   defaulted to false for cones instead of checking hypothesis convexity.
   Fixes block_with_conical_hole (concave conical hole) face orientation.

All 5 cone models now pass full pipeline through stage 4.1.
382 tests pass (was 375)."
```
